        Idea:
v0.14.0 — strict flag-resolution at the gate

## Goal
Remove the silent implicit-acceptance of unresolved blocking flags when the gate recommends PROCEED. Make the gate either resolve every blocking flag explicitly, or pick ITERATE / ESCALATE. Reject rubber-stamp rationale on accept_tradeoff actions, not just on dispute actions.

## Motivation
The current gate logic (v0.13) has two related problems:
1. The gate prompt tells the agent "you may resolve at most 3 flags explicitly per gate call. Any remaining blocking flags are implicitly accepted as tradeoffs if you recommend PROCEED." (prompts/gate.py, around lines 105-114)
2. The handler enforces this by silently auto-marking unresolved blocking flags as accept_tradeoff (handlers.py, around lines 621-635 inside _apply_gate_outcome).
3. The rubber-stamp check (handlers.py around lines 600-607) only validates dispute.evidence — it accepts any accept_tradeoff.rationale including boilerplate.

Net effect: a gate PROCEED can ship with 5 unresolved blocking flags, silently auto-accepted, with zero resistance from the system. The human reviewing the PR sees them in accepted_tradeoffs but the agent was never forced to reason about each one.

## Exact changes

### Change 1: prompts/gate.py
Replace the block that currently reads roughly:
  "You may resolve at most 3 flags explicitly per gate call. Any remaining
  blocking flags are implicitly accepted as tradeoffs if you recommend PROCEED.
  If a flag is structurally unresolvable (e.g., references infrastructure
  outside the repo), you should still PROCEED — do not loop indefinitely
  on flags that cannot be addressed."

with strict language:
  "If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a flag_resolutions entry for
  every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance. If you cannot
  resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision)
  or ESCALATE (human intervention needed). Structurally unresolvable flags
  (infrastructure outside the repo, product decision required) are ESCALATE,
  not PROCEED with a non-answer.

  For each blocking flag:
  - dispute: critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite specific
    file/line/doc — generic statements like 'handled correctly' are invalid.
  - accept_tradeoff: concern is real but accepted as a known limitation.
    Rationale must be specific to this flag — 'acceptable within scope'
    is not valid rationale."

Keep the existing example JSON, but update it to show explicit resolutions for every listed blocking flag (not just a subset).

### Change 2: megaplan/handlers.py _apply_gate_outcome
Replace the silent implicit-acceptance block (the "Implicitly accept remaining blocking flags as tradeoffs" branch around lines 621-635) with:

  - If PROCEED and blocking_unresolved is non-empty:
    - If this is the first attempt for this iteration: reprompt the gate worker once, with the missing flag IDs listed in the new prompt. The existing worker machinery should support this (see _run_worker and the refreshed flag).
    - If reprompt also returns blocking_unresolved: force-downgrade recommendation to ITERATE. Mutate gate_summary in place so the artifact reflects the downgrade. Append to gate_summary["rationale"]: " [Auto-downgraded from PROCEED: N blocking flag(s) not resolved after reprompt]"
    - Return next_step = "revise".
  - Do NOT call update_flags_after_gate with implicit resolutions for unresolved flags. Remove the implicit_resolutions list entirely.

Persist a marker in state or the gate artifact so we can tell whether reprompt already happened this iteration. Either a field on gate_summary (e.g., `reprompted: true`) or a key in state meta. Pick whichever is less invasive.

### Change 3: megaplan/handlers.py rubber-stamp check extension
In the loop around lines 597-608 that processes resolutions:

  - Keep the existing dispute check (skip if evidence is missing or is_rubber_stamp returns True).
  - Add a matching check for accept_tradeoff: skip if rationale is missing or is_rubber_stamp(rationale, strict=True) returns True. A skipped accept_tradeoff means the flag is treated as unresolved and falls through to the Change 2 logic.

### Change 4: tests
Update existing gate tests:
- Test that a PROCEED with 3 resolutions out of 5 blocking flags triggers a reprompt, not silent acceptance.
- Test that a PROCEED still missing flags after reprompt is auto-downgraded to ITERATE, with the appended rationale.
- Test that an accept_tradeoff with rubber-stamp rationale ("acceptable", "fine as is") is rejected.
- Test that an accept_tradeoff with concrete rationale passes.
- Test that PROCEED with every blocking flag resolved (no reprompt needed) still works end-to-end.

Remove any existing test that asserts the implicit-acceptance behavior (that was testing a bug).

### Change 5: version bump
- pyproject.toml: version "0.13.0" -> "0.14.0".
- CHANGELOG.md: new v0.14.0 entry summarizing the above.

## Out of scope (explicitly NOT doing in v0.14.0)
- Do not add a separate per-flag resolve phase.
- Do not add scaffolding-level recurrence counters or divergence detectors — the gate agent already sees iteration history via gate_signals and can reason about recurrence on its own.
- Do not add severity-gated auto-ITERATE rules beyond what the existing logic covers.
- Do not change the flag severity taxonomy (blocking = significant + likely-significant; noted = everything else).
- Do not change the override commands (force-proceed, abort, replan, add-note).
- Do not touch the gate signals computation, preflight checks, or worker infrastructure.

## Branch + commit hygiene
- Create branch `gate-strict-flag-resolution` off main.
- One commit for the code change, one for the test update is fine, or a single atomic commit — reviewer preference.
- Commit message must reference v0.14.0 and summarize the behavior change.
- Open a PR on GitHub against main when done. Do not auto-merge.

## Success criteria (verifiable)
- All existing megaplan tests pass.
- New tests above pass.
- Running the existing test fixture that simulates a gate PROCEED with unresolved flags produces an ITERATE downgrade (not silent acceptance).
- pyproject.toml version is "0.14.0".
- CHANGELOG.md has the new entry.

- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname="hidden md:block">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls.         Idea:
v0.14.0 — strict flag-resolution at the gate

## Goal
Remove the silent implicit-acceptance of unresolved blocking flags when the gate recommends PROCEED. Make the gate either resolve every blocking flag explicitly, or pick ITERATE / ESCALATE. Reject rubber-stamp rationale on accept_tradeoff actions, not just on dispute actions.

## Motivation
The current gate logic (v0.13) has two related problems:
1. The gate prompt tells the agent "you may resolve at most 3 flags explicitly per gate call. Any remaining blocking flags are implicitly accepted as tradeoffs if you recommend PROCEED." (prompts/gate.py, around lines 105-114)
2. The handler enforces this by silently auto-marking unresolved blocking flags as accept_tradeoff (handlers.py, around lines 621-635 inside _apply_gate_outcome).
3. The rubber-stamp check (handlers.py around lines 600-607) only validates dispute.evidence — it accepts any accept_tradeoff.rationale including boilerplate.

Net effect: a gate PROCEED can ship with 5 unresolved blocking flags, silently auto-accepted, with zero resistance from the system. The human reviewing the PR sees them in accepted_tradeoffs but the agent was never forced to reason about each one.

## Exact changes

### Change 1: prompts/gate.py
Replace the block that currently reads roughly:
  "You may resolve at most 3 flags explicitly per gate call. Any remaining
  blocking flags are implicitly accepted as tradeoffs if you recommend PROCEED.
  If a flag is structurally unresolvable (e.g., references infrastructure
  outside the repo), you should still PROCEED — do not loop indefinitely
  on flags that cannot be addressed."

with strict language:
  "If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a flag_resolutions entry for
  every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance. If you cannot
  resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision)
  or ESCALATE (human intervention needed). Structurally unresolvable flags
  (infrastructure outside the repo, product decision required) are ESCALATE,
  not PROCEED with a non-answer.

  For each blocking flag:
  - dispute: critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite specific
    file/line/doc — generic statements like 'handled correctly' are invalid.
  - accept_tradeoff: concern is real but accepted as a known limitation.
    Rationale must be specific to this flag — 'acceptable within scope'
    is not valid rationale."

Keep the existing example JSON, but update it to show explicit resolutions for every listed blocking flag (not just a subset).

### Change 2: megaplan/handlers.py _apply_gate_outcome
Replace the silent implicit-acceptance block (the "Implicitly accept remaining blocking flags as tradeoffs" branch around lines 621-635) with:

  - If PROCEED and blocking_unresolved is non-empty:
    - If this is the first attempt for this iteration: reprompt the gate worker once, with the missing flag IDs listed in the new prompt. The existing worker machinery should support this (see _run_worker and the refreshed flag).
    - If reprompt also returns blocking_unresolved: force-downgrade recommendation to ITERATE. Mutate gate_summary in place so the artifact reflects the downgrade. Append to gate_summary["rationale"]: " [Auto-downgraded from PROCEED: N blocking flag(s) not resolved after reprompt]"
    - Return next_step = "revise".
  - Do NOT call update_flags_after_gate with implicit resolutions for unresolved flags. Remove the implicit_resolutions list entirely.

Persist a marker in state or the gate artifact so we can tell whether reprompt already happened this iteration. Either a field on gate_summary (e.g., `reprompted: true`) or a key in state meta. Pick whichever is less invasive.

### Change 3: megaplan/handlers.py rubber-stamp check extension
In the loop around lines 597-608 that processes resolutions:

  - Keep the existing dispute check (skip if evidence is missing or is_rubber_stamp returns True).
  - Add a matching check for accept_tradeoff: skip if rationale is missing or is_rubber_stamp(rationale, strict=True) returns True. A skipped accept_tradeoff means the flag is treated as unresolved and falls through to the Change 2 logic.

### Change 4: tests
Update existing gate tests:
- Test that a PROCEED with 3 resolutions out of 5 blocking flags triggers a reprompt, not silent acceptance.
- Test that a PROCEED still missing flags after reprompt is auto-downgraded to ITERATE, with the appended rationale.
- Test that an accept_tradeoff with rubber-stamp rationale ("acceptable", "fine as is") is rejected.
- Test that an accept_tradeoff with concrete rationale passes.
- Test that PROCEED with every blocking flag resolved (no reprompt needed) still works end-to-end.

Remove any existing test that asserts the implicit-acceptance behavior (that was testing a bug).

### Change 5: version bump
- pyproject.toml: version "0.13.0" -> "0.14.0".
- CHANGELOG.md: new v0.14.0 entry summarizing the above.

## Out of scope (explicitly NOT doing in v0.14.0)
- Do not add a separate per-flag resolve phase.
- Do not add scaffolding-level recurrence counters or divergence detectors — the gate agent already sees iteration history via gate_signals and can reason about recurrence on its own.
- Do not add severity-gated auto-ITERATE rules beyond what the existing logic covers.
- Do not change the flag severity taxonomy (blocking = significant + likely-significant; noted = everything else).
- Do not change the override commands (force-proceed, abort, replan, add-note).
- Do not touch the gate signals computation, preflight checks, or worker infrastructure.

## Branch + commit hygiene
- Create branch `gate-strict-flag-resolution` off main.
- One commit for the code change, one for the test update is fine, or a single atomic commit — reviewer preference.
- Commit message must reference v0.14.0 and summarize the behavior change.
- Open a PR on GitHub against main when done. Do not auto-merge.

## Success criteria (verifiable)
- All existing megaplan tests pass.
- New tests above pass.
- Running the existing test fixture that simulates a gate PROCEED with unresolved flags produces an ITERATE downgrade (not silent acceptance).
- pyproject.toml version is "0.14.0".
- CHANGELOG.md has the new entry.

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname="hidden md:block">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: i checked the sign-in callback path again. `submissionslayout` already wires `usescoring(..., { onrequiresignin: () => pagemachine.dispatch({ type: 'open_sign_in_modal' }) })` at `src/pages/submissionslayout.tsx:38-40`, and `scorepanel` still has its own `onrequiresignin` prop. the revised step 4.2 is acceptable only if it explicitly rewires that prop to `pagemachine.dispatch`; otherwise removing local modal state would orphan the direct ui callback. the plan hints at this, but it remains easy to misread because the scoring-context callback and the scorepanel prop are separate mechanisms. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the new diff-helper proposal against the repo's existing execution-evidence semantics. `validate_execution_evidence()` in `megaplan/evaluation.py:109-175` uses `git status --short`, so untracked files count as real changed files today, but step 3 proposes `collect_git_diff_patch(project_dir)` via `git diff head`; by git behavior that will not include untracked files, so heavy review could miss exactly the newly created files that execute and review already treat as part of the patch. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: a generated-schema gap remains. `tests/test_schemas.py` reads the repo-root `.megaplan/schemas/review.json` directly via `_review_disk_schema()`, the checked-in local file is currently stale relative to `schemas`, and `ensure_runtime_layout()` in `megaplan/_core/io.py` is the production writer for that file class. the plan says generated schemas must stay aligned, but it never names a regeneration step or a test adjustment for that disk-schema path, so the validation story is still incomplete. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: mobile behavior is still under-prioritized relative to the actual risk in this repo. the user constraint specifically mentions the mobile bottom nav, but the only mobile-specific success criterion left is 'mobile scoring ux is coherent' at `info` level. given that the plan currently removes the working mobile nav, there should be at least a `should` or `must` criterion stating that mobile users retain navigation and scoring controls. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

        Idea:
v0.14.0 — strict flag-resolution at the gate

## Goal
Remove the silent implicit-acceptance of unresolved blocking flags when the gate recommends PROCEED. Make the gate either resolve every blocking flag explicitly, or pick ITERATE / ESCALATE. Reject rubber-stamp rationale on accept_tradeoff actions, not just on dispute actions.

## Motivation
The current gate logic (v0.13) has two related problems:
1. The gate prompt tells the agent "you may resolve at most 3 flags explicitly per gate call. Any remaining blocking flags are implicitly accepted as tradeoffs if you recommend PROCEED." (prompts/gate.py, around lines 105-114)
2. The handler enforces this by silently auto-marking unresolved blocking flags as accept_tradeoff (handlers.py, around lines 621-635 inside _apply_gate_outcome).
3. The rubber-stamp check (handlers.py around lines 600-607) only validates dispute.evidence — it accepts any accept_tradeoff.rationale including boilerplate.

Net effect: a gate PROCEED can ship with 5 unresolved blocking flags, silently auto-accepted, with zero resistance from the system. The human reviewing the PR sees them in accepted_tradeoffs but the agent was never forced to reason about each one.

## Exact changes

### Change 1: prompts/gate.py
Replace the block that currently reads roughly:
  "You may resolve at most 3 flags explicitly per gate call. Any remaining
  blocking flags are implicitly accepted as tradeoffs if you recommend PROCEED.
  If a flag is structurally unresolvable (e.g., references infrastructure
  outside the repo), you should still PROCEED — do not loop indefinitely
  on flags that cannot be addressed."

with strict language:
  "If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a flag_resolutions entry for
  every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance. If you cannot
  resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision)
  or ESCALATE (human intervention needed). Structurally unresolvable flags
  (infrastructure outside the repo, product decision required) are ESCALATE,
  not PROCEED with a non-answer.

  For each blocking flag:
  - dispute: critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite specific
    file/line/doc — generic statements like 'handled correctly' are invalid.
  - accept_tradeoff: concern is real but accepted as a known limitation.
    Rationale must be specific to this flag — 'acceptable within scope'
    is not valid rationale."

Keep the existing example JSON, but update it to show explicit resolutions for every listed blocking flag (not just a subset).

### Change 2: megaplan/handlers.py _apply_gate_outcome
Replace the silent implicit-acceptance block (the "Implicitly accept remaining blocking flags as tradeoffs" branch around lines 621-635) with:

  - If PROCEED and blocking_unresolved is non-empty:
    - If this is the first attempt for this iteration: reprompt the gate worker once, with the missing flag IDs listed in the new prompt. The existing worker machinery should support this (see _run_worker and the refreshed flag).
    - If reprompt also returns blocking_unresolved: force-downgrade recommendation to ITERATE. Mutate gate_summary in place so the artifact reflects the downgrade. Append to gate_summary["rationale"]: " [Auto-downgraded from PROCEED: N blocking flag(s) not resolved after reprompt]"
    - Return next_step = "revise".
  - Do NOT call update_flags_after_gate with implicit resolutions for unresolved flags. Remove the implicit_resolutions list entirely.

Persist a marker in state or the gate artifact so we can tell whether reprompt already happened this iteration. Either a field on gate_summary (e.g., `reprompted: true`) or a key in state meta. Pick whichever is less invasive.

### Change 3: megaplan/handlers.py rubber-stamp check extension
In the loop around lines 597-608 that processes resolutions:

  - Keep the existing dispute check (skip if evidence is missing or is_rubber_stamp returns True).
  - Add a matching check for accept_tradeoff: skip if rationale is missing or is_rubber_stamp(rationale, strict=True) returns True. A skipped accept_tradeoff means the flag is treated as unresolved and falls through to the Change 2 logic.

### Change 4: tests
Update existing gate tests:
- Test that a PROCEED with 3 resolutions out of 5 blocking flags triggers a reprompt, not silent acceptance.
- Test that a PROCEED still missing flags after reprompt is auto-downgraded to ITERATE, with the appended rationale.
- Test that an accept_tradeoff with rubber-stamp rationale ("acceptable", "fine as is") is rejected.
- Test that an accept_tradeoff with concrete rationale passes.
- Test that PROCEED with every blocking flag resolved (no reprompt needed) still works end-to-end.

Remove any existing test that asserts the implicit-acceptance behavior (that was testing a bug).

### Change 5: version bump
- pyproject.toml: version "0.13.0" -> "0.14.0".
- CHANGELOG.md: new v0.14.0 entry summarizing the above.

## Out of scope (explicitly NOT doing in v0.14.0)
- Do not add a separate per-flag resolve phase.
- Do not add scaffolding-level recurrence counters or divergence detectors — the gate agent already sees iteration history via gate_signals and can reason about recurrence on its own.
- Do not add severity-gated auto-ITERATE rules beyond what the existing logic covers.
- Do not change the flag severity taxonomy (blocking = significant + likely-significant; noted = everything else).
- Do not change the override commands (force-proceed, abort, replan, add-note).
- Do not touch the gate signals computation, preflight checks, or worker infrastructure.

## Branch + commit hygiene
- Create branch `gate-strict-flag-resolution` off main.
- One commit for the code change, one for the test update is fine, or a single atomic commit — reviewer preference.
- Commit message must reference v0.14.0 and summarize the behavior change.
- Open a PR on GitHub against main when done. Do not auto-merge.

## Success criteria (verifiable)
- All existing megaplan tests pass.
- New tests above pass.
- Running the existing test fixture that simulates a gate PROCEED with unresolved flags produces an ITERATE downgrade (not silent acceptance).
- pyproject.toml version is "0.14.0".
- CHANGELOG.md has the new entry.

Revise the plan.\"\n+                )\n+                result = \"blocked\"\n+                next_step = \"revise\"\n+                summary = f\"Gate recommendation {gate_summary['recommendation']}: {gate_summary['rationale']}\"\n+        _merge_resolution_tradeoffs_into_payload(gate_summary, worker.payload)\n+        gate_hash = _write_json_artifact(plan_dir, \"gate.json\", gate_summary)\n+        debt_entries_added = 0\n+        if gate_summary[\"recommendation\"] == \"PROCEED\":\n+            debt_entries_added = _record_gate_debt_entries(root, state, gate_summary, worker.payload)\n         # Store last_gate AFTER _apply_gate_outcome — the outcome may override\n         # the recommendation (e.g. PROCEED → ITERATE when flags are unresolved).\n         _store_last_gate(state, gate_summary)\n*** End Patch\n"
}

        Idea:
v0.14.0 — strict flag-resolution at the gate

## Goal
Remove the silent implicit-acceptance of unresolved blocking flags when the gate recommends PROCEED. Make the gate either resolve every blocking flag explicitly, or pick ITERATE / ESCALATE. Reject rubber-stamp rationale on accept_tradeoff actions, not just on dispute actions.

## Motivation
The current gate logic (v0.13) has two related problems:
1. The gate prompt tells the agent "you may resolve at most 3 flags explicitly per gate call. Any remaining blocking flags are implicitly accepted as tradeoffs if you recommend PROCEED." (prompts/gate.py, around lines 105-114)
2. The handler enforces this by silently auto-marking unresolved blocking flags as accept_tradeoff (handlers.py, around lines 621-635 inside _apply_gate_outcome).
3. The rubber-stamp check (handlers.py around lines 600-607) only validates dispute.evidence — it accepts any accept_tradeoff.rationale including boilerplate.

Net effect: a gate PROCEED can ship with 5 unresolved blocking flags, silently auto-accepted, with zero resistance from the system. The human reviewing the PR sees them in accepted_tradeoffs but the agent was never forced to reason about each one.

## Exact changes

### Change 1: prompts/gate.py
Replace the block that currently reads roughly:
  "You may resolve at most 3 flags explicitly per gate call. Any remaining
  blocking flags are implicitly accepted as tradeoffs if you recommend PROCEED.
  If a flag is structurally unresolvable (e.g., references infrastructure
  outside the repo), you should still PROCEED — do not loop indefinitely
  on flags that cannot be addressed."

with strict language:
  "If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a flag_resolutions entry for
  every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance. If you cannot
  resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision)
  or ESCALATE (human intervention needed). Structurally unresolvable flags
  (infrastructure outside the repo, product decision required) are ESCALATE,
  not PROCEED with a non-answer.

  For each blocking flag:
  - dispute: critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite specific
    file/line/doc — generic statements like 'handled correctly' are invalid.
  - accept_tradeoff: concern is real but accepted as a known limitation.
    Rationale must be specific to this flag — 'acceptable within scope'
    is not valid rationale."

Keep the existing example JSON, but update it to show explicit resolutions for every listed blocking flag (not just a subset).

### Change 2: megaplan/handlers.py _apply_gate_outcome
Replace the silent implicit-acceptance block (the "Implicitly accept remaining blocking flags as tradeoffs" branch around lines 621-635) with:

  - If PROCEED and blocking_unresolved is non-empty:
    - If this is the first attempt for this iteration: reprompt the gate worker once, with the missing flag IDs listed in the new prompt. The existing worker machinery should support this (see _run_worker and the refreshed flag).
    - If reprompt also returns blocking_unresolved: force-downgrade recommendation to ITERATE. Mutate gate_summary in place so the artifact reflects the downgrade. Append to gate_summary["rationale"]: " [Auto-downgraded from PROCEED: N blocking flag(s) not resolved after reprompt]"
    - Return next_step = "revise".
  - Do NOT call update_flags_after_gate with implicit resolutions for unresolved flags. Remove the implicit_resolutions list entirely.

Persist a marker in state or the gate artifact so we can tell whether reprompt already happened this iteration. Either a field on gate_summary (e.g., `reprompted: true`) or a key in state meta. Pick whichever is less invasive.

### Change 3: megaplan/handlers.py rubber-stamp check extension
In the loop around lines 597-608 that processes resolutions:

  - Keep the existing dispute check (skip if evidence is missing or is_rubber_stamp returns True).
  - Add a matching check for accept_tradeoff: skip if rationale is missing or is_rubber_stamp(rationale, strict=True) returns True. A skipped accept_tradeoff means the flag is treated as unresolved and falls through to the Change 2 logic.

### Change 4: tests
Update existing gate tests:
- Test that a PROCEED with 3 resolutions out of 5 blocking flags triggers a reprompt, not silent acceptance.
- Test that a PROCEED still missing flags after reprompt is auto-downgraded to ITERATE, with the appended rationale.
- Test that an accept_tradeoff with rubber-stamp rationale ("acceptable", "fine as is") is rejected.
- Test that an accept_tradeoff with concrete rationale passes.
- Test that PROCEED with every blocking flag resolved (no reprompt needed) still works end-to-end.

Remove any existing test that asserts the implicit-acceptance behavior (that was testing a bug).

### Change 5: version bump
- pyproject.toml: version "0.13.0" -> "0.14.0".
- CHANGELOG.md: new v0.14.0 entry summarizing the above.

## Out of scope (explicitly NOT doing in v0.14.0)
- Do not add a separate per-flag resolve phase.
- Do not add scaffolding-level recurrence counters or divergence detectors — the gate agent already sees iteration history via gate_signals and can reason about recurrence on its own.
- Do not add severity-gated auto-ITERATE rules beyond what the existing logic covers.
- Do not change the flag severity taxonomy (blocking = significant + likely-significant; noted = everything else).
- Do not change the override commands (force-proceed, abort, replan, add-note).
- Do not touch the gate signals computation, preflight checks, or worker infrastructure.

## Branch + commit hygiene
- Create branch `gate-strict-flag-resolution` off main.
- One commit for the code change, one for the test update is fine, or a single atomic commit — reviewer preference.
- Commit message must reference v0.14.0 and summarize the behavior change.
- Open a PR on GitHub against main when done. Do not auto-merge.

## Success criteria (verifiable)
- All existing megaplan tests pass.
- New tests above pass.
- Running the existing test fixture that simulates a gate PROCEED with unresolved flags produces an ITERATE downgrade (not silent acceptance).
- pyproject.toml version is "0.14.0".
- CHANGELOG.md has the new entry.

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/CHANGELOG.md\n@@\n # Changelog\n \n+## v0.14.0 — 2026-04-15\n+\n+### Strict gate flag resolution\n+\n+The gate no longer silently accepts unresolved blocking flags on a PROCEED recommendation. PROCEED now requires explicit `flag_resolutions` for every blocking flag, and a retry is issued once when the first response still leaves blocking blockers unresolved.\n+\n+- **No implicit acceptance**: unresolved blocking flags now trigger a single gate reprompt instead of being auto-marked as accepted tradeoffs.\n+- **Auto-downgrade on retry failure**: if the retry still leaves blocking flags unresolved, the gate artifact is rewritten as `ITERATE` with an auto-downgrade rationale note and `reprompted: true`.\n+- **Stricter tradeoff validation**: `accept_tradeoff` entries now require concrete, flag-specific rationale; rubber-stamp phrases are rejected the same way weak dispute evidence is rejected.\n+- **Debt derived from explicit resolutions**: accepted tradeoff debt entries now come from validated `flag_resolutions` rather than fallback unresolved-flag recording.\n+- **Gate test coverage refreshed**: existing gate debt tests now follow the strict-resolution contract, and new tests cover reprompt success, reprompt downgrade, rubber-stamp rejection, no-reprompt happy path, and resolution-derived debt recording.\n+\n ## v0.12.0 — 2026-04-15\n \n ### Auto driver\n*** End Patch\n"
}

> AGENT

The failures are from the default fixture carrying more than one blocking flag. The new strict gate logic is doing the right thing: tests that only resolved a single flag were still leaving other blockers open and legitimately triggering the retry path. I’m tightening the fixtures so each test controls the exact blocking-flag count it intends to exercise.

        Idea:
v0.14.0 — strict flag-resolution at the gate

## Goal
Remove the silent implicit-acceptance of unresolved blocking flags when the gate recommends PROCEED. Make the gate either resolve every blocking flag explicitly, or pick ITERATE / ESCALATE. Reject rubber-stamp rationale on accept_tradeoff actions, not just on dispute actions.

## Motivation
The current gate logic (v0.13) has two related problems:
1. The gate prompt tells the agent "you may resolve at most 3 flags explicitly per gate call. Any remaining blocking flags are implicitly accepted as tradeoffs if you recommend PROCEED." (prompts/gate.py, around lines 105-114)
2. The handler enforces this by silently auto-marking unresolved blocking flags as accept_tradeoff (handlers.py, around lines 621-635 inside _apply_gate_outcome).
3. The rubber-stamp check (handlers.py around lines 600-607) only validates dispute.evidence — it accepts any accept_tradeoff.rationale including boilerplate.

Net effect: a gate PROCEED can ship with 5 unresolved blocking flags, silently auto-accepted, with zero resistance from the system. The human reviewing the PR sees them in accepted_tradeoffs but the agent was never forced to reason about each one.

## Exact changes

### Change 1: prompts/gate.py
Replace the block that currently reads roughly:
  "You may resolve at most 3 flags explicitly per gate call. Any remaining
  blocking flags are implicitly accepted as tradeoffs if you recommend PROCEED.
  If a flag is structurally unresolvable (e.g., references infrastructure
  outside the repo), you should still PROCEED — do not loop indefinitely
  on flags that cannot be addressed."

with strict language:
  "If you recommend PROCEED, you MUST provide a flag_resolutions entry for
  every blocking flag. There is no implicit acceptance. If you cannot
  resolve every blocking flag, choose ITERATE (send back for revision)
  or ESCALATE (human intervention needed). Structurally unresolvable flags
  (infrastructure outside the repo, product decision required) are ESCALATE,
  not PROCEED with a non-answer.

  For each blocking flag:
  - dispute: critique is factually wrong. Evidence must cite specific
    file/line/doc — generic statements like 'handled correctly' are invalid.
  - accept_tradeoff: concern is real but accepted as a known limitation.
    Rationale must be specific to this flag — 'acceptable within scope'
    is not valid rationale."

Keep the existing example JSON, but update it to show explicit resolutions for every listed blocking flag (not just a subset).

### Change 2: megaplan/handlers.py _apply_gate_outcome
Replace the silent implicit-acceptance block (the "Implicitly accept remaining blocking flags as tradeoffs" branch around lines 621-635) with:

  - If PROCEED and blocking_unresolved is non-empty:
    - If this is the first attempt for this iteration: reprompt the gate worker once, with the missing flag IDs listed in the new prompt. The existing worker machinery should support this (see _run_worker and the refreshed flag).
    - If reprompt also returns blocking_unresolved: force-downgrade recommendation to ITERATE. Mutate gate_summary in place so the artifact reflects the downgrade. Append to gate_summary["rationale"]: " [Auto-downgraded from PROCEED: N blocking flag(s) not resolved after reprompt]"
    - Return next_step = "revise".
  - Do NOT call update_flags_after_gate with implicit resolutions for unresolved flags. Remove the implicit_resolutions list entirely.

Persist a marker in state or the gate artifact so we can tell whether reprompt already happened this iteration. Either a field on gate_summary (e.g., `reprompted: true`) or a key in state meta. Pick whichever is less invasive.

### Change 3: megaplan/handlers.py rubber-stamp check extension
In the loop around lines 597-608 that processes resolutions:

  - Keep the existing dispute check (skip if evidence is missing or is_rubber_stamp returns True).
  - Add a matching check for accept_tradeoff: skip if rationale is missing or is_rubber_stamp(rationale, strict=True) returns True. A skipped accept_tradeoff means the flag is treated as unresolved and falls through to the Change 2 logic.

### Change 4: tests
Update existing gate tests:
- Test that a PROCEED with 3 resolutions out of 5 blocking flags triggers a reprompt, not silent acceptance.
- Test that a PROCEED still missing flags after reprompt is auto-downgraded to ITERATE, with the appended rationale.
- Test that an accept_tradeoff with rubber-stamp rationale ("acceptable", "fine as is") is rejected.
- Test that an accept_tradeoff with concrete rationale passes.
- Test that PROCEED with every blocking flag resolved (no reprompt needed) still works end-to-end.

Remove any existing test that asserts the implicit-acceptance behavior (that was testing a bug).

### Change 5: version bump
- pyproject.toml: version "0.13.0" -> "0.14.0".
- CHANGELOG.md: new v0.14.0 entry summarizing the above.

## Out of scope (explicitly NOT doing in v0.14.0)
- Do not add a separate per-flag resolve phase.
- Do not add scaffolding-level recurrence counters or divergence detectors — the gate agent already sees iteration history via gate_signals and can reason about recurrence on its own.
- Do not add severity-gated auto-ITERATE rules beyond what the existing logic covers.
- Do not change the flag severity taxonomy (blocking = significant + likely-significant; noted = everything else).
- Do not change the override commands (force-proceed, abort, replan, add-note).
- Do not touch the gate signals computation, preflight checks, or worker infrastructure.

## Branch + commit hygiene
- Create branch `gate-strict-flag-resolution` off main.
- One commit for the code change, one for the test update is fine, or a single atomic commit — reviewer preference.
- Commit message must reference v0.14.0 and summarize the behavior change.
- Open a PR on GitHub against main when done. Do not auto-merge.

## Success criteria (verifiable)
- All existing megaplan tests pass.
- New tests above pass.
- Running the existing test fixture that simulates a gate PROCEED with unresolved flags produces an ITERATE downgrade (not silent acceptance).
- pyproject.toml version is "0.14.0".
- CHANGELOG.md has the new entry.

- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the mobile action-surface plan is technically incorrect for the current component structure. `submissioncarouselcard` hides `footercontent` below the `md` breakpoint (`src/components/submissioncarouselcard.tsx:219`), while `submissionscarouselpage` also hides the score panel on mobile via `asidecontent={<div classname="hidden md:block">...` and passes `hideactions` to `scorepanel` at `src/pages/submissionscarouselpage.tsx:250-263,347`. reusing that pattern for entrypage would leave mobile users with no navigation controls and no scoring controls. If a test fails and its ID appears in baseline_test_failures, it is pre-existing — do not scope-creep into fixing it. If baseline_test_failures is null, the baseline could not be captured; use your judgment but err on the side of assuming failures are regressions. You MUST still re-run the FULL test suite with your changes applied — pre-existing failures do not excuse skipping verification. Never narrow to individual test functions and stop.
        - If this batch includes the final verification task, write a short script that reproduces the exact bug described in the task, run it to confirm the fix resolves it, then delete the script.

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: /tmp/repro_gate_downgrade.py\n+from __future__ import annotations\n+\n+import json\n+import tempfile\n+from pathlib import Path\n+\n+import megaplan\n+import megaplan.workers\n+from megaplan.workers import WorkerResult\n+from tests.test_megaplan import ensure_blocking_flags, make_args_factory\n+\n+\n+def gate_worker_result(session_id: str) -> tuple[WorkerResult, str, str, bool]:\n+    return (\n+        WorkerResult(\n+            payload={\n+                \"recommendation\": \"PROCEED\",\n+                \"rationale\": \"Proceed without explicitly resolving the blockers.\",\n+                \"signals_assessment\": \"The blockers remain unresolved.\",\n+                \"warnings\": [],\n+                \"settled_decisions\": [],\n+                \"flag_resolutions\": [],\n+                \"accepted_tradeoffs\": [],\n+            },\n+            raw_output=\"{}\",\n+            duration_ms=1,\n+            cost_usd=0.0,\n+            session_id=session_id,\n+        ),\n+        \"claude\",\n+        \"persistent\",\n+        False,\n+    )\n+\n+\n+def main() -> None:\n+    original = megaplan.workers.run_step_with_worker\n+    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp_dir:\n+        root = Path(tmp_dir) / \"root\"\n+        project_dir = root / \"project\"\n+        project_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n+        make_args = make_args_factory(project_dir)\n+\n+        init = megaplan.handle_init(root, make_args(name=\"repro-plan\", robustness=\"standard\"))\n+        plan_name = init[\"plan\"]\n+        megaplan.handle_plan(root, make_args(plan=plan_name))\n+        megaplan.handle_critique(root, make_args(plan=plan_name))\n+\n+        plan_dir = megaplan.plans_root(root) / plan_name\n+        ensure_blocking_flags(plan_dir, 2)\n+\n+        results = iter(\n+            [\n+                gate_worker_result(\"repro-gate-1\"),\n+                gate_worker_result(\"repro-gate-2\"),\n+            ]\n+        )\n+        megaplan.workers.run_step_with_worker = lambda *args, **kwargs: next(results)\n+        try:\n+            response = megaplan.handle_gate(root, make_args(plan=plan_name))\n+        finally:\n+            megaplan.workers.run_step_with_worker = original\n+\n+    assert response[\"recommendation\"] == \"ITERATE\", response\n+    assert response[\"reprompted\"] is True, response\n+    assert \"[Auto-downgraded from PROCEED:\" in response[\"rationale\"], response\n+    print(json.dumps(response, indent=2))\n+\n+\n+if __name__ == \"__main__\":\n+    main()\n*** End Patch\n"
}
